Moldavski – Slovenian" lang="sl" hreflang="sl" class="interlanguage-link-target"><span>Slovenščina</span></a></li><li class="interlanguage-link interwiki-sr mw-list-item"><a href="https://sr.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%A1%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%84%D0%B0%D0%BD_III_%D0%9C%D0%BE%D0%BB%D0%B4%D0%B0%D0%B2%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B8" title="Стефан III Молдавски – Serbian" lang="sr" hreflang="sr" class="interlanguage-link-target"><span>Српски / srpski</span></a></li><li class="interlanguage-link interwiki-sh mw-list-item"><a href="https://sh.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_III_Moldavski" title="Stefan III Moldavski – Serbo-Croatian" lang="sh" hreflang="sh" class="interlanguage-link-target"><span>Srpskohrvatski / српскохрватски</span></a></li><li class="interlanguage-link interwiki-fi mw-list-item"><a href="https://fi.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tapani_III_Suuri" title="Tapani III Suuri – Finnish" lang="fi" hreflang="fi" class="interlanguage-link-target"><span>Suomi</span></a></li><li class="interlanguage-link interwiki-sv mw-list-item"><a href="https://sv.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_III_av_Moldavien" title="Stefan III av Moldavien – Swedish" lang="sv" hreflang="sv" class="interlanguage-link-target"><span>Svenska</span></a></li><li class="interlanguage-link interwiki-tr mw-list-item"><a href="https://tr.wikipedia.org/wiki/III._%C5%9Etefan" title="III. Ştefan – Turkish" lang="tr" hreflang="tr" class="interlanguage-link-target"><span>Türkçe</span></a></li><li class="interlanguage-link interwiki-uk mw-list-item"><a href="https://uk.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%A8%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%84%D0%B0%D0%BD_III" title="Штефан III – Ukrainian" lang="uk" hreflang="uk" class="interlanguage-link-target"><span>Українська</span></a></li><li class="interlanguage-link interwiki-vi mw-list-item"><a href="https://vi.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C8%98tefan_III_c%E1%BB%A7a_Moldavia" title="Ștefan III của Moldavia – Vietnamese" lang="vi" hreflang="vi" class="interlanguage-link-target"><span>Tiếng Việt</span></a></li><li class="interlanguage-link interwiki-zh mw-list-item"><a href="https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%96%AF%E7%89%B9%E5%87%A1%E4%B8%89%E4%B8%96" title="斯特凡三世 – Chinese" lang="zh" hreflang="zh" class="interlanguage-link-target"><span>中文</span></a></li></ul>
			<div class="after-portlet after-portlet-lang"><span class="wb-langlinks-edit wb-langlinks-link"><a href="https://www.wikidata.org/wiki/Special:EntityPage/Q218134#sitelinks-wikipedia" title="Edit interlanguage links" class="wbc-editpage">Edit links</a></span></div>

					<div id="contentSub"><div id="mw-content-subtitle"><span class="mw-redirectedfrom">(Redirected from <a href="/w/index.php?title=Stephen_III_of_Moldavia&amp;redirect=no" class="mw-redirect" title="Stephen III of Moldavia">Stephen III of Moldavia</a>)</span></div></div>
					
					
					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">"Ștefan cel Mare" redirects here. For other uses, see <a href="/wiki/%C8%98tefan_cel_Mare_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="Ștefan cel Mare (disambiguation)">Ștefan cel Mare (disambiguation)</a>.</div>
<div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Prince of Moldavia from 1457 to 1504</div>
<p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1066479718">.mw-parser-output .infobox-subbox{padding:0;border:none;margin:-3px;width:auto;min-width:100%;font-size:100%;clear:none;float:none;background-color:transparent}.mw-parser-output .infobox-3cols-child{margin:auto}.mw-parser-output .infobox .navbar{font-size:100%}body.skin-minerva .mw-parser-output .infobox-header,body.skin-minerva .mw-parser-output .infobox-subheader,body.skin-minerva .mw-parser-output .infobox-above,body.skin-minerva .mw-parser-output .infobox-title,body.skin-minerva .mw-parser-output .infobox-image,body.skin-minerva .mw-parser-output .infobox-full-data,body.skin-minerva .mw-parser-output .infobox-below{text-align:center}</style><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above fn" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/4f/Humorstefan.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="236" data-file-height="239" /></a><div class="infobox-caption" style="line-height:normal;padding-bottom:0.2em;padding-top:0.2em;">Miniature from the 1473 <i>Gospel</i> at <a href="/wiki/Humor_Monastery" title="Humor Monastery">Humor href="/wiki/Evdochia_of_Kiev" title="Evdochia of Kiev">Evdochia of Kiev</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Maria_of_Mangup" title="Maria of Mangup">Maria of Mangup</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Maria_Voichi%C8%9Ba" href="#Family">more...</a></i></span></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Alexandru_of_Moldavia" title="Alexandru of Moldavia">Alexandru</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Bogdan_III_the_One-Eyed" title="Bogdan III the One-Eyed">Bogdan III</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Petru_Rare%C8%99" title="Petru Rareș">Petru Rareș</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Dynasty" title="Dynasty">Dynasty</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/House_of_Mu%C8%99at" class="mw-redirect" title="House of Mușat">Mușat</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Father</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Bogdan_II_of_Moldavia" title="Bogdan II of Moldavia">Bogdan II of Moldavia</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Mother</th><td class="infobox-data">Maria Oltea</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religion</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Orthodox Christian</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Stephen III of Moldavia</b>, most commonly known as <b>Stephen the Great</b> (<a href="/wiki/Romanian_language" title="Romanian language">Romanian</a>: <i lang="ro">Ștefan cel Mare</i>; <span class="nowrap"><small>pronunciation&#58;&#160;</small></span><span class="IPA" lang="ro-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">&#91;</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">ˈ</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">ʃ</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">t</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">e</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">f</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">a</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">n</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">&#160;</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">tʃ</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">e</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">l</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">&#160;</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">ˈ</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">m</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">a</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">r</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">e</a><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Romanian" title="Help:IPA/Romanian">&#93;</a></span>; died on 2 July 1504), was <a href="/wiki/List_of_rulers_of_Moldavia" title="List of rulers of Moldavia">Voivode (or Prince)</a> of <a href="/wiki/Moldavia" title="Moldavia">Moldavia</a> from 1457 to 1504. He was the son of and co-ruler with <a href="/wiki/Bogdan_II_of_Moldavia" title="Bogdan II of Moldavia">Bogdan II</a>, who was murdered in 1451 in a conspiracy organized by his brother and Stephen's uncle <a href="/wiki/Peter_Aaron" title="Peter Aaron">Peter III Aaron</a>, who took the throne. Stephen fled to <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_(1301%E2%80%931526)" title="Kingdom of Hungary (1301–1526)">Hungary</a>, and later to  <a href="/wiki/Wallachia" title="Wallachia">Wallachia</a>; with the support of <a href="/wiki/Vlad_the_Impaler" title="Vlad the Impaler">Vlad III Țepeș</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_rulers_of_Wallachia" title="List of rulers of Wallachia">Voivode of Wallachia</a>, he returned to Moldavia, forcing Aaron to seek refuge in <a href="/wiki/Crown_of_the_Kingdom_of_Poland" title="Crown of the Kingdom of Poland">Poland</a> in the summer of 1457. <a href="/wiki/Teoctist_I_of_Moldavia" title="Teoctist I of Moldavia">Teoctist I</a>, <a href="/wiki/Metropolis_of_Moldavia_and_Bukovina" title="Metropolis of Moldavia and Bukovina">Metropolitan of Moldavia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anointing" title="Anointing">anointed</a> Stephen prince. He attacked Poland and prevented <a href="/wiki/Casimir_IV_Jagiellon" title="Casimir IV Jagiellon">Casimir IV Jagiellon</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_Polish_monarchs" title="List of Polish monarchs">King of Poland</a>, from supporting Peter Aaron, but eventually acknowledged Casimir's suzerainty in 1459.
</p><p>Stephen decided to recapture Chilia (now <a href="/wiki/Kiliia" title="Kiliia">Kiliia</a> in Ukraine), an important port on the Danube, which brought him into conflict with Hungary and Wallachia. He besieged the town during the Ottoman invasion of Wallachia in 1462, but was seriously wounded during the siege. Two years later, he captured the town. He promised support to the leaders of the <a href="/wiki/Unio_Trium_Nationum" title="Unio Trium Nationum">Three Nations of Transylvania</a> against <a href="/wiki/Matthias_Corvinus" title="Matthias Corvinus">Matthias Corvinus</a>, <a href="/wiki/King_of_Hungary" title="King of Hungary">King of Hungary</a>, in 1467. Corvinus invaded Moldavia, but Stephen defeated him in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Baia" title="Battle of Baia">Battle of Baia</a>. Peter Aaron attacked Moldavia with Hungarian support in December 1470, but was also defeated by Stephen and executed, along with the <a href="/wiki/Boyars_of_Moldavia_and_Wallachia" title="Boyars of Moldavia and Wallachia">Moldavian boyars</a> who still endorsed him. Stephen restored old fortresses and built new ones, which improved Moldavia's defence system as well as strengthened central administration. Ottoman expansion threatened Moldavian ports in the region of the <a href="/wiki/Black_Sea" title="Black Sea">Black Sea</a>. In 1473, Stephen stopped paying tribute (<i><a href="/wiki/Hara%C3%A7" title="Haraç">haraç</a></i>) to the <a href="/wiki/List_of_sultans_of_the_Ottoman_Empire" title="List of sultans of the Ottoman Empire">Ottoman sultan</a> and launched a <a href="/wiki/Moldavian%E2%80%93Ottoman_Wars" title="Moldavian–Ottoman Wars">series of campaigns against Wallachia</a> in order to replace its rulers – who had accepted Ottoman suzerainty – with his protégés. However, each prince who seized the throne with Stephen's support was soon forced to pay homage to the sultan.
</p><p>Stephen eventually defeated a large Ottoman army in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Vaslui" title="Battle of Vaslui">Battle of Vaslui</a> in 1475. He was referred to as <i><a href="/wiki/Athleta_Christi" title="Athleta Christi">Athleta Christi</a></i> ("Champion of Christ") by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Sixtus_IV" title="Pope Sixtus IV">Pope Sixtus IV</a>, even though Moldavia's hopes for military support went unfulfilled. The following year, Ottoman Sultan <a href="/wiki/Mehmed_the_Conqueror" class="mw-redirect" title="Mehmed the Conqueror">Mehmed II</a> routed Stephen in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Valea_Alb%C4%83" title="Battle of Valea Albă">Battle of Valea Albă</a>, but the lack of provisions and the outbreak of a plague forced him to withdraw from Moldavia. Taking advantage of a truce with Matthias Corvinus, the Ottomans captured Chilia, their <a href="/wiki/Crimean_Tatars" title="Crimean Tatars">Crimean Tatar</a> allies Cetatea Albă (now <a href="/wiki/Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi" title="Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi">Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi</a> in Ukraine) in 1484. Although Corvinus granted two <a href="/wiki/Transylvania" title="Transylvania">Transylvanian</a> estates to Stephen, the Moldavian prince paid homage to Casimir, who promised to support him to regain Chilia and Cetatea Albă. Stephen's efforts to capture the two ports ended in failure. From 1486, he again paid a yearly tribute to the Ottomans. During the following years, dozens of stone churches and monasteries were built in Moldavia, which contributed to the development of a specific Moldavian architecture.
</p><p>Casimir IV's successor, <a href="/wiki/John_I_Albert" title="John I Albert">John I Albert</a>, wanted to grant Moldavia to his younger brother, <a href="/wiki/Sigismund_I_the_Old" title="Sigismund I the Old">Sigismund</a>, but Stephen's diplomacy prevented him from invading Moldavia for years. John Albert <a href="/wiki/Moldavian_Campaign_(1497%E2%80%931499)" class="mw-redirect" title="Moldavian Campaign (1497–1499)">attacked Moldavia</a> in 1497, but Stephen and his Hungarian and Ottoman allies routed the Polish army in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Cosmin_Forest" title="Battle of the Cosmin Forest">Battle of the Cosmin Forest</a>. Stephen again tried to recapture Chilia and Cetatea Albă, but had to acknowledge the loss of the two ports to the Ottomans in 1503. During his last years, his son and co-ruler <a href="/wiki/Bogdan_III_the_One-Eyed" title="Bogdan III the One-Eyed">Bogdan III</a> played an active role in government. Stephen's long rule represented a period of stability in the history of Moldavia. From the 16th century onwards both his subjects and foreigners remembered him as a great ruler. Modern Romanians regard him as one of their greatest national heroes, although he also endures as a cult figure in <a href="/wiki/Moldovenism" title="Moldovenism">Moldovenism</a>. After the <a href="/wiki/Romanian_Orthodox_Church" title="Romanian Orthodox Church">Romanian Orthodox Church</a> canonized him in 1992, he is venerated as "Stephen the Great and Holy" (<i>Ștefan cel Mare și Sfânt</i>).
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stephen_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Moldova1483.jpg" class="image"><img alt="A map depicting Moldavia bordered by the river Dniester, the Black Sea and the Carpathian Mountains" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Moldova1483.jpg/220px-Moldova1483.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="201" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Moldova1483.jpg/330px-Moldova1483.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Moldova1483.jpg/440px-Moldova1483.jpg 2x" data-file-width="657" data-file-height="599" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Moldova1483.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Moldavia" title="Moldavia">Principality of Moldavia</a> in 1483</div></div></div>
<p>Stephen was the son of <a href="/wiki/Bogdan_II_of_Moldavia" title="Bogdan II of Moldavia">Bogdan</a>, who was a son of <a href="/wiki/Alexander_I_of_Moldavia" title="Alexander I of Moldavia">Alexander the Good</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_rulers_of_Moldavia" title="List of rulers of Moldavia">Prince of Moldavia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPăun2016131_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPăun2016131-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> Stephen's mother, Maria Oltea,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPăun2016131_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPăun2016131-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEIliescu200679_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEIliescu200679-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> was most probably related to the princes of <a href="/wiki/Wallachia" title="Wallachia">Wallachia</a>, according to historian <a href="/wiki/Radu_Florescu" title="Radu Florescu">Radu Florescu</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally198966_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally198966-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The date of Stephen's birth is unknown,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptowPopa1996190_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptowPopa1996190-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> though historians estimate that he was born between 1433 and 1440.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrezianuSpânu2007338_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrezianuSpânu2007338-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014220_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014220-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> One church <a href="/wiki/Diptych" title="Diptych">diptych</a> records that he had five siblings: brothers Ioachim, Ioan, Christea; and sisters Sorea and Maria.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEIliescu200679_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEIliescu200679-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939–40_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939–40-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> Some of Stephen's biographers hypothesize that Cârstea Arbore, father of the statesman <a href="/wiki/Luca_Arbore" title="Luca Arbore">Luca Arbore</a>, was the prince's fourth brother, or that Cârstea was the same as Ioachim.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013136_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013136-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> These links with the high-ranking <a href="/wiki/Boyars_of_Moldavia_and_Wallachia" title="Boyars of Moldavia and Wallachia">Moldavian boyars</a> are known to have been preserved through matrimonial connections: Maria, who died in 1485, was the wife of Șendrea, gatekeeper of <a href="/wiki/Suceava" title="Suceava">Suceava</a>; Stephen's other brother-in-law, Isaia, also held high office at his court.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044,_8_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044,_8-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196940_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196940-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The death of Alexander the Good in 1432 gave rise to a succession crisis that lasted more than two decades.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005256_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005256-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997103_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997103-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Stephen's father seized the throne in 1449 after defeating one of his relatives with the support of <a href="/wiki/John_Hunyadi" title="John Hunyadi">John Hunyadi</a>, Regent-Governor of Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005256_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005256-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199134_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199134-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Stephen was styled <i><a href="/wiki/Voivode" title="Voivode">voivode</a></i> in his father's charters, showing that he had been made his father's heir and co-ruler.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERezachievici200718_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERezachievici200718-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201431,_212_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201431,_212-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Bogdan acknowledged the suzerainty of Hunyadi in 1450.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199134–35_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199134–35-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Stephen fled to Hungary after <a href="/wiki/Peter_Aaron" title="Peter Aaron">Peter III Aaron</a> (who was also Alexander the Good's son) murdered Bogdan in October 1451.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally198966_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally198966-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow200059_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow200059-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014212_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014212-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Vlad Țepeș (who had lived in Moldavia during Bogdan II's reign) invaded Wallachia and seized the throne with the support of Hunyadi in 1456.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow200058,_61_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow200058,_61-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Stephen either accompanied Vlad to Wallachia during the military campaign or joined him after Vlad became the ruler of Wallachia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow200098_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow200098-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> According to reports from the 1480s, Stephen spent part of that interval in <a href="/wiki/Br%C4%83ila" title="Brăila">Brăila</a>, where he fathered an illegitimate son, Mircea.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938,_40–41_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938,_40–41-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> With the assistance of Vlad, Stephen stormed into Moldavia at the head of an army 6,000 strong in the spring of 1457.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938_5-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow200099_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow200099-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERezachievici200718–19_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERezachievici200718–19-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201434_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201434-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> According to Moldavian chronicles, "men from the Lower Country" (the southern region of Moldavia) joined him.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938_5-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow200099_24-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow200099-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea19965_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea19965-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The 17th-century <a href="/wiki/Grigore_Ureche" title="Grigore Ureche">Grigore Ureche</a> wrote: "Stephen routed Peter Aaron <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Dolje%C8%99ti_and_Orbic" title="Battle of Doljești and Orbic">at Doljești</a> on 12 April, but Peter Aaron left Moldavia for Poland only after Stephen inflicted a second defeat on him at Orbic."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014212_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014212-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow200099_24-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow200099-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERezachievici200718–19_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERezachievici200718–19-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Reign">Reign</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stephen_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Reign">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_campaigns">Early campaigns</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stephen_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Early campaigns">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>One widely accepted theory, based on Ureche, states that an assembly of boyars and Orthodox clergymen <a href="/wiki/Acclamatio" title="Acclamatio">acclaimed</a> Stephen the ruler of Moldavia at Direptate, a meadow near Suceava. According to scholar Constantin Rezachievici, this elective custom has no precedent before the 17th century, and appears superfluous in Stephen's case; he argues that it was a legend fabricated by Ureche.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERezachievici200717–18,_20–30_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERezachievici200717–18,_20–30-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> While this election remains uncertain, various historians agree that <a href="/wiki/Teoctist_I_of_Moldavia" title="Teoctist I of Moldavia">Teoctist I</a>, <a href="/wiki/Metropolis_of_Moldavia_and_Bukovina" title="Metropolis of Moldavia and Bukovina">Metropolitan of Moldavia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anointing" title="Anointing">anointed</a> Stephen prince.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERezachievici200725,_27_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERezachievici200725,_27-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005266_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005266-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008106–108,_138_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008106–108,_138-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> To emphasize the sacred nature of his rule, Stephen styled himself "<a href="/wiki/By_the_Grace_of_God" title="By the Grace of God">By the Grace of God</a>, ... Stephen voivode, lord (or <i><a href="/wiki/Hospodar" title="Hospodar">hospodar</a></i>) of the Moldavian lands" on 13 September 1457.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201432–33_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201432–33-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> His use of Christian devices for legitimization overlapped with a troubled context for Moldavian Orthodoxy: the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Florence" title="Council of Florence">attempted Catholic–Orthodox union</a> had divided the <a href="/wiki/Byzantine_Rite" title="Byzantine Rite">Byzantine Rite</a> churches into supporters and dissidents; likewise, the <a href="/wiki/Fall_of_Constantinople" title="Fall of Constantinople">Fall of Constantinople</a> had encouraged local bishops to consider themselves independent of the <a href="/wiki/Ecumenical_Patriarch_of_Constantinople" title="Ecumenical Patriarch of Constantinople">Patriarchy</a>. There is a long-standing dispute about whether Teoctist was a dissenter, belonging to one of the several emancipated Orthodox jurisdictions, or a loyalist of <a href="/wiki/Isidore_II_of_Constantinople" title="Isidore II of Constantinople">Patriarch Isidore</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> Historian Dan Ioan Mureșan argues that the evidence is for the latter option, because Moldavia appears on the list of Patriarchate jurisdictions, and because Stephen, though he tested the Patriarch by sometimes using imperial titles such as <i><a href="/wiki/Tsar" title="Tsar">tsar</a></i> by 1473, was never threatened with <a href="/wiki/Excommunication" title="Excommunication">excommunication</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008102–180_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008102–180-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As one of his earliest actions as prince, Stephen attacked Poland to prevent Casimir IV from supporting Peter Aaron in 1458.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201438,_213_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201438,_213-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> This first military campaign "established his credentials as a military commander of stature", according to historian Jonathan Eagles.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201438_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201438-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> However, he wanted to avoid prolonged conflict with Poland, because the recapture of Chilia was his principal aim.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199143_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199143-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Chilia was an important port on the Danube that <a href="/wiki/Petru_III_of_Moldavia" title="Petru III of Moldavia">Peter III of Moldavia</a> had surrendered to Hungary in 1448.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043_29-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199133_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199133-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He signed a treaty with Poland on the river <a href="/wiki/Dniester" title="Dniester">Dniester</a> on 4 April 1459.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043_29-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199143_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199143-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> He acknowledged the suzerainty of Casimir IV and promised to support Poland against <a href="/wiki/Tatars" title="Tatars">Tatar</a> marauders.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043_29-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213_40-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Casimir in turn pledged to protect Stephen against his enemies and to forbid Peter Aaron from returning to Moldavia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043_29-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213_40-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199635_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199635-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Peter Aaron subsequently left Poland for <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Hungary</a> and settled in <a href="/wiki/Sz%C3%A9kely_Land" title="Székely Land">Székely Land</a>, <a href="/wiki/Transylvania" title="Transylvania">Transylvania</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043_29-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005266_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005266-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Stephen invaded Székely Land multiple times in 1461.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043_29-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213_40-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Matthias_Corvinus" title="Matthias Corvinus">Matthias Corvinus</a>, <a href="/wiki/King_of_Hungary" title="King of Hungary">King of Hungary</a>, decided to support Peter Aaron, giving him shelter in his capital at <a href="/wiki/Buda" title="Buda">Buda</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213_40-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> In 1462, Stephen underscored his wish for good relations with the Ottoman Empire, expelling from Moldavia the <a href="/wiki/Franciscans" title="Franciscans">Franciscans</a>, who were agitating for a united church and a crusade.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008110_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008110-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Stephen continued to pay the yearly tribute to the Ottoman Empire initiated by his predecessor.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005266_31-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005266-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199143_38-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199143-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008110_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008110-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> He also made a new agreement with Poland in Suceava on 2 March 1462, promising to personally swear <a href="/wiki/Fealty" title="Fealty">fealty</a> to Casimir IV if the king required it.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199143–44_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199143–44-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> This treaty declared that Casimir was the sole suzerain of Moldavia, prohibiting Stephen from alienating Moldavian territories without his authorization.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000139_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000139-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199144_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199144-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> It also obliged Stephen to recapture the Moldavian territories that had been lost, obviously in reference to Chilia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000139_44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000139-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199144_45-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199144-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Written sources evidence that the relationship between Stephen and Vlad Țepeș became tense in early 1462.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000136_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000136-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> On 2 April 1462, the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Genoa" title="Republic of Genoa">Genoese</a> governor of Caffa (now <a href="/wiki/Feodosia" title="Feodosia">Feodosia</a> in <a href="/wiki/Crimea" title="Crimea">Crimea</a>) informed Casimir IV of Poland that Stephen had attacked Wallachia while Vlad Țepeș was waging war against the Ottomans.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000138_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000138-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The <a href="/wiki/List_of_sultans_of_the_Ottoman_Empire" title="List of sultans of the Ottoman Empire">Ottoman Sultan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mehmed_the_Conqueror" class="mw-redirect" title="Mehmed the Conqueror">Mehmed II</a>, later invaded Wallachia in June 1462.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000130_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000130-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Mehmed's secretary, <a href="/wiki/Tursun_Beg" title="Tursun Beg">Tursun Beg</a>, recorded that Vlad Țepeș had to station 7,000 soldiers near the Wallachian-Moldavian frontier during the sultan's invasion to "protect his country against his Moldavian enemies".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000140_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000140-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Both Tursun and <a href="/wiki/Laonikos_Chalkokondyles" title="Laonikos Chalkokondyles">Laonikos Chalkokondyles</a> note that Stephen's troops were loyal to Mehmed, and directly involved in the invasion.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008110_42-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008110-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Taking advantage of the presence of the Ottoman fleet at the <a href="/wiki/Danube_Delta" title="Danube Delta">Danube Delta</a>, Stephen also laid siege to Chilia in late June.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000140_49-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000140-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989148–149_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989148–149-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> According to Domenico Balbi, the Venetian envoy in <a href="/wiki/Istanbul" title="Istanbul">Istanbul</a>, Stephen and the Ottomans besieged the fortress for eight days, but they could not capture it, because the "Hungarian garrison and Țepeș's 7,000 men" defeated them, killing "many Turks".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000140_49-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000140-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989149_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989149-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Stephen was seriously wounded during the siege, suffering an injury on his left calf, or his left foot, that would never heal his entire life.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043_29-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989149_51-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989149-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Consolidation">Consolidation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stephen_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Consolidation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

title="Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi">Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi</a> in Ukraine)</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Manastirea_Putna.jpg" class="image"><img alt="A church in a rose garden" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Manastirea_Putna.jpg/200px-Manastirea_Putna.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="133" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Manastirea_Putna.jpg/300px-Manastirea_Putna.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Manastirea_Putna.jpg/400px-Manastirea_Putna.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3456" data-file-height="2304" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/Putna_Monastery" title="Putna Monastery">Putna Monastery</a>, founded in 1466 by Stephen</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Stephen again laid siege to Chilia on 24 January 1465.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213_40-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000142_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000142-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Moldavian_military_forces" title="Moldavian military forces">Moldavian army</a> bombarded the fortress for two days, forcing the garrison to surrender on 25 or 26 January.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000142_52-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000142-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> The sultan's vassal, <a href="/wiki/Radu_the_Handsome" title="Radu the Handsome">Radu the Handsome</a>, Voivode of Wallachia, had also laid claim to Chilia, thus the capture of the port gave rise to conflicts not only with Hungary, but also with Wallachia and the Ottoman Empire.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199638_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199638-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199144–45_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199144–45-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005266–267_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005266–267-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> In 1465<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213_40-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> or earlier,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043,_4_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20043,_4-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Stephen peacefully regained the fortress of <a href="/wiki/Khotyn" title="Khotyn">Hotin</a> (now Khotyn in Ukraine) on the Dniester from the Poles. To commemorate the capture of Chilia, Stephen ordered the construction of the Church of the Assumption of the Mother of God in a <a href="/wiki/Glade_(geography)" title="Glade (geography)">glade</a> on the <a href="/wiki/Putna_(Suceava)" title="Putna (Suceava)">Putna River</a> in 1466.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201494_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201494-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> It became the central monument of <a href="/wiki/Putna_Monastery" title="Putna Monastery">Putna Monastery</a>, extended by Stephen in 1467, when he donated the village of <a href="/wiki/Vicovu_de_Sus" title="Vicovu de Sus">Vicov</a>, and finally consecrated in September 1470.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044,_5_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044,_5-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At Matthias Corvinus' instance, the <a href="/wiki/Diet_of_Hungary" title="Diet of Hungary">Diet of Hungary</a> abolished all previous exemptions relating to the tax known as the "chamber's profit".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKubinyi200882_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKubinyi200882-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> The leaders of the <a href="/wiki/Unio_Trium_Nationum" title="Unio Trium Nationum">Three Nations of Transylvania</a> who regarded the reform as an infringement of their privileges declared on 18 August 1467 that they were ready to fight to defend their liberties.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKubinyi200882_59-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKubinyi200882-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Stephen promised support to them,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213_40-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> but they yielded to Corvinus without resistance after the king marched to Transylvania.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKubinyi200883_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKubinyi200883-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> Corvinus invaded Moldavia and captured Baia, <a href="/wiki/Bac%C4%83u" title="Bacău">Bacău</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roman,_Romania" title="Roman, Romania">Roman</a> and <a href="/wiki/T%C3%A2rgu_Neam%C8%9B" title="Târgu Neamț">Târgu Neamț</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213_40-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Stephen assembled his army and launched a crushing defeat on the invaders in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Baia" title="Battle of Baia">Battle of Baia</a> on 15 December.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044_3-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001302_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001302-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213–214_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014213–214-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> This episode was presented in contemporary Hungarian chronicles as a defeat of Stephen's armies.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETiron201276,_79_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETiron201276,_79-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> However, Corvinus, who received wounds in the battle, could only escape from the battlefield with the help of <a href="/wiki/Boyars_of_Moldavia_and_Wallachia" title="Boyars of Moldavia and Wallachia">Moldavian boyars</a> who had joined him.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044_3-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> A group of boyars rose up against Stephen in the Lower Country,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199625_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199625-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> but he had 20 boyars and 40 other landowners captured and executed before the end of the year.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Stephen again swore loyalty to Casimir IV in the presence of the Polish envoy in Suceava on 28 July 1468.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199144_45-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199144-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He conducted raids against Transylvania between 1468 and 1471.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> When Casimir came to <a href="/wiki/Lviv" title="Lviv">Lviv</a> in February 1469 to personally receive his homage, Stephen did not go to meet him.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> In the same year or in early 1470, Tatars invaded Moldavia, but Stephen routed them in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Lipnic" title="Battle of Lipnic">Battle of Lipnic</a> near the Dniester.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrezianuSpânu2007xxvi_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrezianuSpânu2007xxvi-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> To strengthen the defence system along the river, Stephen decided to erect new fortresses at <a href="/wiki/Old_Orhei" title="Old Orhei">Old Orhei</a> and <a href="/wiki/Soroca" title="Soroca">Soroca</a> around the same time.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrezianuSpânu2007xxvi_68-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrezianuSpânu2007xxvi-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201442_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201442-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> A Wallachian army laid siege to Chilia, but it could not force the Moldavian garrison to surrender.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Matthias Corvinus sent peace proposals to Stephen.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146_66-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> His envoys sought Casimir IV's advice on Corvinus' proposals at the <a href="/wiki/Sejm_of_the_Kingdom_of_Poland" title="Sejm of the Kingdom of Poland">Sejm</a> (or general assembly) of Poland at <a href="/wiki/Piotrk%C3%B3w_Trybunalski" title="Piotrków Trybunalski">Piotrków Trybunalski</a> in late 1469.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146_66-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Stephen invaded Wallachia and destroyed Brăila and <a href="/wiki/Ora%C8%99ul_de_Floci" title="Orașul de Floci">Târgul de Floci</a> (the two most important Wallachian centres of commerce on the Danube) in February 1470.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045_67-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199642_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199642-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECristea2016316–324_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECristea2016316–324-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Peter Aaron hired <a href="/wiki/Sz%C3%A9kelys" title="Székelys">Székely</a> troops and broke into Moldavia in December 1470,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> but his attack was probably anticipated by Stephen.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044–5_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044–5-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> The voivode defeated his rival near Târgu Neamț.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Peter Aaron fell captive in the battlefield. He and his Moldavian supporters, among them Stephen's <i><a href="/wiki/Vornic" title="Vornic">vornic</a></i> and brother-in-law, Isaia,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196940_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196940-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> and the chancellor Alexa, were executed on the orders of Stephen.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045_67-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199624–25_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199624–25-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Radu the Fair also invaded Moldavia, but Stephen defeated him at Soci on 7 March 1471.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199642_70-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199642-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> Reportedly, he killed all but two of the Wallachian noblemen he captured in battle.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECristea2016316_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECristea2016316-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The relationship between Casimir IV and Matthias Corvinus became tense in early 1471.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146–47_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146–47-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> After Stephen failed to support Poland, Casimir IV dispatched an embassy to Moldavia, insisting that Stephen should comply with his obligations.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146_66-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199641_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199641-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> Stephen met the Polish envoys in <a href="/wiki/Vaslui" title="Vaslui">Vaslui</a> on 13 July, reminding them of the hostile acts Polish noblemen committed along the border and demanded the extradition of the Moldavian boyars who had fled to Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146_66-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> In parallel, he sent his own envoys to Hungary to start negotiations with Corvinus.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146_66-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199146-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He granted commercial privileges to <a href="/wiki/Transylvanian_Saxons" title="Transylvanian Saxons">Saxon</a> merchants from the Transylvanian town of Corona (now <a href="/wiki/Bra%C8%99ov" title="Brașov">Brașov</a>) on 3 January 1472.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045_67-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199147_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199147-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Wars_with_Mehmed_II">Wars with Mehmed II</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stephen_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Wars with Mehmed II">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Vaslui" title="Battle of Vaslui">Battle of Vaslui</a> and <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Neam%C8%9B_Citadel" title="Siege of Neamț Citadel">Siege of Neamț Citadel</a></div>
<p>The Ottomans put pressure on Stephen to abandon Chilia and <a href="/wiki/Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi" title="Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi">Cetatea Albă</a> (now Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi in Ukraine) in the early 1470s.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005267_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005267-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Instead of obeying their demands, Stephen declined to send the yearly tribute to the <a href="/wiki/Sublime_Porte" title="Sublime Porte">Sublime Porte</a> in 1473.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005267_78-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005267-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> From 1472, he had friendly contacts with <a href="/wiki/Uzun_Hasan" title="Uzun Hasan">Uzun Hasan</a>, sultan of <a href="/wiki/Aq_Qoyunlu" title="Aq Qoyunlu">Aq Qoyunlu</a>, plotting an anti-Ottoman coordination.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045_67-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> Taking advantage of Mehmed's war against Uzun in Anatolia, Stephen invaded Wallachia to replace Radu the Fair, an Ottoman-installed Muslim convert and vassal, with his protégé, <a href="/wiki/Basarab_the_Old" title="Basarab the Old">Basarab III Laiotă</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989165_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989165-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> He routed the Wallachian army at <a href="/wiki/R%C3%A2mnicu_S%C4%83rat" title="Râmnicu Sărat">Râmnicu Sărat</a> in a battle that lasted for three days from 18 to 20 November 1473.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989165_80-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989165-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> Four days later, the Moldavian army captured <a href="/wiki/Bucharest" title="Bucharest">Bucharest</a> and Stephen placed Basarab on the throne.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989165_80-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989165-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> However, Radu regained Wallachia with Ottoman support before the end of the year.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214_64-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014214-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Basarab again expelled Radu from Wallachia in 1475, but the Ottomans once more assisted him to return.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> The Wallachians took revenge by plundering some parts of Moldavia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> To restore Basarab, Stephen launched a new campaign to Wallachia in October, forcing Radu to flee from the principality.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215_81-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mehmed II ordered <a href="/wiki/Had%C4%B1m_Suleiman_Pasha_(governor_of_Rumelia)" title="Hadım Suleiman Pasha (governor of Rumelia)">Hadım Suleiman Pasha</a>, <a href="/wiki/Beylerbey" title="Beylerbey">Beylerbey</a> (or governor) of <a href="/wiki/Rumelia_Eyalet" title="Rumelia Eyalet">Rumelia</a>, to invade Moldavia – an Ottoman army of about 120,000 strong broke into Moldavia in late 1475.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Wallachian troops also joined the Ottomans, while Stephen received support from Poland and Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215_81-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShaw197668_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw197668-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> Outnumbered three to one by the invaders, Stephen was forced to retreat.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914_82-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997116_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997116-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He joined battle with Hadım Suleiman Pasha at Podul Înalt (or the High Bridge) near Vaslui on 10 January 1475.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914_82-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199648_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199648-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Before the battle, he had sent his buglers to hide behind the enemy fronts.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914_82-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> When they suddenly sounded their horns, they caused such a panic among the invaders that they fled from the battlefield.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914_82-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Over the next three days, hundreds of Ottoman soldiers were massacred and the survivors retreated from Moldavia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECristea2016316_74-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECristea2016316-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914_82-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Vaslui_Battle_map.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Vaslui_Battle_map.svg/220px-Vaslui_Battle_map.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="288" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Vaslui_Battle_map.svg/330px-Vaslui_Battle_map.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Vaslui_Battle_map.svg/440px-Vaslui_Battle_map.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="660" data-file-height="863" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Vaslui_Battle_map.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Stephen's tactics in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Vaslui" title="Battle of Vaslui">Battle of Vaslui</a></div></div></div>
<p>Stephen's victory in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Vaslui" title="Battle of Vaslui">Battle of Vaslui</a> was "arguably one of the biggest European victories over the Ottomans", according to historian <a href="/wiki/Alexander_Mikaberidze" title="Alexander Mikaberidze">Alexander Mikaberidze</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914_82-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMikaberidze2011914-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Mara_Brankovi%C4%87" title="Mara Branković">Mara Branković</a>, Mehmed II's stepmother, stated the Ottomans "had never suffered a greater defeat".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Stephen sent letters to the European rulers to seek their support against the Ottomans, reminding them that Moldavia was "the Gateway of Christianity" and "the bastion of Hungary and Poland and the guardian of these kingdoms".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215_81-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997116_84-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997116-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECândea2004141_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECândea2004141-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pope_Sixtus_IV" title="Pope Sixtus IV">Pope Sixtus IV</a> praised him as <i><a href="/wiki/Athleta_Christi" title="Athleta Christi">Verus christiane fidei athleta</a></i> ("The true defender of the Christian faith").<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECândea2004141_86-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECândea2004141-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> However, neither the Pope, nor any other European power, sent material support to Moldavia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215_81-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997116_84-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997116-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Stephen was also approaching Mehmed with peace offers. According to disputed reports by the chronicler <a href="/wiki/Jan_D%C5%82ugosz" title="Jan Długosz">Jan Długosz</a>, he was also playing down the invasion as the deed of "some fugitives and brigands" whom the Sultan would want to punish.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECristea2016317–318,_325_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECristea2016317–318,_325-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Meanwhile, Stephen's brother-in-law, Alexander, seized the <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Theodoro" title="Principality of Theodoro">Principality of Theodoro</a> in the Crimea at the head of a Moldavian army.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201446_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201446-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199650_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199650-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> Stephen also decided to expel his former protégé, Basarab Laiotă, from Wallachia, because Basarab had supported the Ottomans during their invasion of Moldavia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000160_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000160-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He made an alliance with Matthias Corvinus in July,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199650_89-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199650-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> persuading him to release Basarab's rival, Vlad Țepeș, who had been imprisoned in Hungary in 1462.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000160_90-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000160-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Stephen and Vlad made an agreement to put an end to the conflicts between Moldavia and Wallachia, but Corvinus did not support them to invade Wallachia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000160_90-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000160-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The Ottomans occupied the Principality of Theodoro and the Genoese colonies in the Crimea before the end of 1475.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201446_88-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201446-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Stephen ordered the execution of the Ottoman prisoners in Moldavia to take vengeance for the massacre of Alexander of Theodoro and his Moldavian retainers.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201446_88-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201446-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Thereafter the Venetians, who had waged war against the Ottomans since 1463, regarded Stephen as their principal ally.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199644,_52_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199644,_52-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> With their support, Stephen's envoys tried to persuade the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> to finance Stephen's war directly, instead of sending the funds to Matthias Corvinus.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199652_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199652-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Signoria_of_Venice" title="Signoria of Venice">Signoria of Venice</a> emphasized, "No one should fail to understand the extent to which Stephen could influence the evolution of events, one way or another", referring to his pre-eminent role in the anti-Ottoman alliance.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199652_92-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199652-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Mehmed II personally commanded a new invasion against Moldavia in the summer of 1476.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005267_78-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005267-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997116_84-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997116-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources.
