(July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> This force included 12,000 Wallachians under Laiotă, and a retinue of Moldavians under a certain Alexandru, who claimed to be Stephen's brother.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046_79-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20046-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Crimean_Tatars" title="Crimean Tatars">Crimean Tatars</a> were the first to break into Moldavia at the Sultan's order, but Stephen routed them.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShaw197668_83-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw197668-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199653_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199653-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> He also persuaded the Tatars of the <a href="/wiki/Great_Horde" title="Great Horde">Great Horde</a> to break into the Crimea, forcing the Crimean Tatars to withdraw from Moldavia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199653_93-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199653-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> The Sultan invaded Moldavia in late June 1476.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShaw197668_83-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw197668-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199653_93-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199653-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005268_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005268-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Himself supported by troops sent by Corvinus,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005268_95-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005268-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> Stephen adopted a <a href="/wiki/Scorched_earth" title="Scorched earth">scorched earth</a> policy, but could not avoid a pitched battle.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShaw197668_83-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw197668-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> He suffered a defeat in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Valea_Alb%C4%83" title="Battle of Valea Albă">Battle of Valea Albă</a> at <a href="/wiki/R%C4%83zboieni,_Neam%C8%9B" title="Războieni, Neamț">Războieni</a> on 26 July and had to seek refuge in Poland, but the Ottomans could not capture the fortress of Suceava, and similarly <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Neam%C8%9B_Citadel" title="Siege of Neamț Citadel">failed before Neamț</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215_81-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005268_95-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005268-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047_94-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> The lack of sufficient provisions and an outbreak of <a href="/wiki/Cholera" title="Cholera">cholera</a> in the Ottoman camp forced Mehmed to leave Moldavia, enabling the voivode to return from Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215_81-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997116-117_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997116-117-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Folk tradition claims that Stephen had also been pledged a new army with the free peasantry of <a href="/wiki/Putna_County" title="Putna County">Putna County</a>, grouped around the seven sons of a local lady, Tudora "Baba" Vrâncioaia. This contingent reportedly attacked the Ottomans' flank at <a href="/wiki/Odobe%C8%99ti" title="Odobești">Odobești</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHârnea197949–57_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHârnea197949–57-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEIliescu200667–69_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEIliescu200667–69-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Another account, repeated by Ureche, is that Maria Oltea forced her son back into battle, pushing him to either return victorious or die.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECristea2016343_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECristea2016343-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Byzantine historian <a href="/wiki/George_Sphrantzes" title="George Sphrantzes">George Sphrantzes</a> concluded that Mehmed II "had suffered more defeats than victories" during the invasion of Moldavia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000162_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000162-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> From summer 1475, during an interlude in the rivalry between Poland and Hungary, Stephen swore his allegiance to the latter.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPilat2010125_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPilat2010125-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> With Hungarian support, Stephen and Vlad Țepeș invaded Wallachia, forcing Basarab Laiotă to flee in November 1476.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047_94-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000162_100-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000162-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Stephen returned to Moldavia, leaving Moldavian troops behind for Vlad's protection.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989173–175_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally1989173–175-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> The Ottomans invaded Wallachia to restore Basarab Laiotă.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047_94-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000166_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000166-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Țepeș and his Moldavian retainers were massacred before 10 January 1477.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptow2000166_103-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptow2000166-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Stephen again broke into Wallachia and replaced Basarab Laiotă with <a href="/wiki/Basarab_the_Young" title="Basarab the Young">Basarab IV the Younger</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047_94-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215_81-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014215-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Stephen sent his envoys to Rome and Venice to persuade the Christian powers to continue the war against the Ottomans.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047_94-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199657_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199657-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> He and Venice also wanted to involve the Great Horde in the anti-Ottoman coalition, but the Poles were unwilling to allow the Tatars to cross their territories.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199657_104-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199657-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> To strengthen his international position, Stephen signed a new treaty with Poland on 22 January 1479, promising to personally swear fealty to Casimir IV in Colomea (now <a href="/wiki/Kolomyia" title="Kolomyia">Kolomyia</a> in Ukraine) if the king  specifically demanded it.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Venice and the Ottoman Empire made peace in the same month; Hungary and Poland in April.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149_105-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> After Basarab the Younger paid homage to the sultan, Stephen had to seek reconciliation with the Ottomans.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149_105-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> In May 1480, he promised to renew the annual tribute that he had stopped paying in 1473.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149_105-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Taking advantage of the peace, Stephen made preparations to a new confrontation with the Ottoman Empire.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149_105-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He again invaded Wallachia and replaced Basarab the Younger with one Mircea, possibly Stephen's own son,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196940–41_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196940–41-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> but Basarab regained Wallachia with Ottoman support.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047_94-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> The Wallachians and their Ottoman allies broke into Moldavia in the spring of 1481.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216_107-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Wars_with_Bayezid_II">Wars with Bayezid II</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stephen_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Wars with Bayezid II">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Ottoman_War_(1485%E2%80%931503)" title="Polish–Ottoman War (1485–1503)">Polish–Ottoman War (1485–1503)</a></div>
<p>Mehmed II died in 1481.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShaw197670_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw197670-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> The conflict between his two sons, <a href="/wiki/Bayezid_II" title="Bayezid II">Bayezid II</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cem_Sultan" title="Cem Sultan">Cem</a>, enabled Stephen to break into Wallachia and the Ottoman Empire in June.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShaw197670,_72_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw197670,_72-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> He routed Basarab the Younger at <a href="/wiki/R%C3%A2mnicu_V%C3%A2lcea" title="Râmnicu Vâlcea">Râmnicu Vâlcea</a> and placed Vlad Țepeș's half-brother,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally198945_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlorescuMcNally198945-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Vlad_C%C4%83lug%C4%83rul" title="Vlad Călugărul">Vlad Călugărul</a>, on the throne.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047–8_111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047–8-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216_107-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECristea2016338_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECristea2016338-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> After Basarab the Younger returned with Ottoman support, Stephen made a last attempt to secure his influence in Wallachia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216_107-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> He again led his army to Wallachia and defeated Basarab the Younger, who died in the battle.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216_107-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> Although Vlad the Monk was restored, he was soon forced to accept the Sultan's suzerainty.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216_107-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> Anticipating a new Ottoman attack, Stephen fortified his frontier with Wallachia and entered an alliance with <a href="/wiki/Ivan_III_of_Russia" class="mw-redirect" title="Ivan III of Russia">Ivan III of Russia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchy_of_Moscow" title="Grand Duchy of Moscow">Grand Prince of Moscow</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048_113-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>...since [Stephen the Great] has ruled in Moldavia he has not liked any ruler of Wallachia. He did not wish to live with [Radu the Fair], nor with [Basarab Laiotă], nor with me. I do not know who can live with him.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8201;<cite>Basarab the Younger's 1481 letter to the councilors of <a href="/wiki/Sibiu" title="Sibiu">Sibiu</a><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997118_114-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997118-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup></cite></div></blockquote><p><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Matthias Corvinus signed a five-year truce with Bayezid II in October 1483.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPilat2010125_101-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPilat2010125-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKubinyi2008112_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKubinyi2008112-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001308_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001308-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> The truce applied to all Moldavia, with the exception of the ports.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216_107-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> Bayezid invaded Moldavia and captured Chilia on 14 or 15 July 1484.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShaw197673_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw197673-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEParry197658_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEParry197658-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048_113-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> His vassal, <a href="/wiki/Me%C3%B1li_I_Giray" title="Meñli I Giray">Meñli I Giray</a>, also broke into Moldavia and seized Cetatea Albă on 3 August.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShaw197673_117-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw197673-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEParry197658_118-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEParry197658-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> The capture of the two ports secured the Ottomans' control of the Black Sea.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005268_95-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005268-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShaw197673_117-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw197673-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKohn2007_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKohn2007-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Bayezid left Moldavia only after Stephen personally came to pay homage to him.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShaw197673_117-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw197673-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> Although this prostration was largely without effect on Moldavian independence,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGemil201339–40_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGemil201339–40-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> the loss of Chilia and Cetatea Albă put an end to the Moldavian control of important trading routes.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201459_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201459-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Corvinus was unwilling to break his own truce with Bayezid, having tacit Ottoman backing for his own <a href="/wiki/Austrian%E2%80%93Hungarian_War_(1477%E2%80%931488)" title="Austrian–Hungarian War (1477–1488)">war in the west</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPilat2010125–126_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPilat2010125–126-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> However, he granted his vassal a territorial gift in Transylvania, comprising the domains of <a href="/wiki/Ciceu-Giurge%C8%99ti" title="Ciceu-Giurgești">Ciceu</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cetatea_de_Balt%C4%83" title="Cetatea de Baltă">Cetatea de Baltă</a>. According to various interpretations, this exchange occurred in or after 1484, and was meant to compensate Stephen for the loss of his ports.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149_105-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216_107-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014216-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECernovodeanu1977118_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECernovodeanu1977118-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDiaconescu201391–96_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDiaconescu201391–96-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> Medievalist Marius Diaconescu dates the lease of Cetatea to 1482, when Corvinus agreed to give Stephen a place of refuge, should Moldavia fall to the Ottomans, while Ciceu only became Stephen's castle in 1489.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDiaconescu201396–110_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDiaconescu201396–110-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> Both citadels were on land confiscated after conflicts between the Three Nations and Corvinus. Ciceu had been a fief of the Losonczi family, under litigation, while Cetatea had been a special domain of the <a href="/wiki/Voivode_of_Transylvania" title="Voivode of Transylvania">Voivode of Transylvania</a>, whose last titular owner before Stephen was John Pongrác of Dengeleg.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDiaconescu201394–98_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDiaconescu201394–98-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By then, <a href="/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Ottoman_War_(1485%E2%80%931503)" title="Polish–Ottoman War (1485–1503)">war between the Poles and the Ottomans</a> was in preparation, with clashes between the two sides occurring in 1484.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKohn2007_119-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKohn2007-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Scholar Șerban Papacostea notes that Casimir IV had always remained neutral during Stephen's conflicts with the Ottomans, but the Ottoman control of the mouths of the <a href="/wiki/Dnieper" title="Dnieper">Dnieper</a> and the Danube threatened Poland. The king, Papacostea argues, also wanted to strengthen his suzerainty over Moldavia, which helped him decide to intervene in the conflict on Stephen's behalf.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199659_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199659-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> Casimir formed<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKohn2007_119-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKohn2007-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> or joined with<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBain190829_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBain190829-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> an anti-Ottoman league, which, in 1485, had also gathered reluctant support from the <a href="/wiki/State_of_the_Teutonic_Order" title="State of the Teutonic Order">Teutonic Knights</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMaasing2015362_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaasing2015362-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> Historians provide different readings of the issue: according to <a href="/wiki/Robert_Nisbet_Bain" title="Robert Nisbet Bain">Robert Nisbet Bain</a>, Casimir's intervention also drove the Ottomans out of Moldavia;<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBain190829_128-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBain190829-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> Veniamin Ciobanu however argues that the Polish involvement<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The time period mentioned near this tag is ambiguous. (August 2017)">when?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> remained non-military, purely diplomatic.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149–50_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149–50-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Casimir then marched on Colomea with 20,000 troops.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKohn2007_119-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKohn2007-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBain190829_128-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBain190829-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPilat2010127_131-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPilat2010127-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> To secure his support, Stephen also went to Colomea and swore fealty to him on 12 September 1485.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199659_127-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199659-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEParry197658,_60_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEParry197658,_60-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPilat2010127,_129_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPilat2010127,_129-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149–50_130-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199149–50-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048,_9_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048,_9-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> The ceremony took place in a tent, but its curtains were drawn aside at the moment when Stephen was on his knees before Casimir.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201460_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201460-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> Three days after Stephen's oath of fealty, Casimir IV pledged that he would not acknowledge the capture of Chilia and Cetatea Albă by the Ottomans without Stephen's consent.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiobanu199150_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiobanu199150-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> During Stephen's visit in Poland, the Ottomans broke into Moldavia and sacked Suceava.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048_113-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> They also tried to place a pretender, Peter Hronoda, on the throne.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048_113-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201460_135-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201460-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199624,_59_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199624,_59-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMărculeț2006_139-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMărculeț2006-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Stephen returned from Poland and defeated the invaders with Polish assistance at <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_C%C4%83tl%C4%83buga" title="Battle of Cătlăbuga">Cătlăbuga Lake</a> in November.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048_113-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEParry197658_118-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEParry197658-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201460,_217_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201460,_217-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPilat2010127–128_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPilat2010127–128-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> He again confronted the Ottomans at <a href="/wiki/%C8%98cheia,_Suceava" title="Șcheia, Suceava">Șcheia</a> in March 1486, but could not recapture Chilia and Cetatea Albă.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048_113-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201460,_217_140-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201460,_217-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> He narrowly escaped with his life, reportedly after being helped by the Aprod Purice, whom tradition identifies as patriarch of the <a href="/wiki/Movile%C8%99ti" title="Movilești">Movilești</a> family.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGorovei197364–65_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGorovei197364–65-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> Historian Vasile Mărculeț agrees with Ottoman sources in noting that Șcheia was not a military victory for Moldavia, but overall a relative success for his enemy, <a href="/wiki/Skender_Pasha" title="Skender Pasha">Skender Pasha</a>. Moldavians reported winning the day only because they narrowly avoided disaster; and because Hronoda, recognized a voivode by dissenting boyars, was captured and beheaded.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMărculeț2006_139-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMărculeț2006-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> In the end, Stephen signed a three-year truce with the Porte, promising to pay the yearly tribute to the Sultan.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048_113-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEParry197658,_60_132-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEParry197658,_60-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217_137-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005269_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005269-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPilat2010132,_135_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPilat2010132,_135-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Conflicts_with_Poland">Conflicts with Poland</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stephen_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Conflicts with Poland">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:%C5%BDygimont_Stary._%D0%96%D1%8B%D0%B3%D1%96%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%A1%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%8B_(H._D%C3%BCrer,_1530).jpg" class="image"><img alt="Portrait of a man in profile, holding a parchment" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/%C5%BDygimont_Stary._%D0%96%D1%8B%D0%B3%D1%96%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%A1%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%8B_%28H._D%C3%BCrer%2C_1530%29.jpg/220px-%C5%BDygimont_Stary._%D0%96%D1%8B%D0%B3%D1%96%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%A1%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%8B_%28H._D%C3%BCrer%2C_1530%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="300" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/%C5%BDygimont_Stary._%D0%96%D1%8B%D0%B3%D1%96%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%A1%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%8B_%28H._D%C3%BCrer%2C_1530%29.jpg/330px-%C5%BDygimont_Stary._%D0%96%D1%8B%D0%B3%D1%96%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%A1%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%8B_%28H._D%C3%BCrer%2C_1530%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/%C5%BDygimont_Stary._%D0%96%D1%8B%D0%B3%D1%96%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%A1%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%8B_%28H._D%C3%BCrer%2C_1530%29.jpg/440px-%C5%BDygimont_Stary._%D0%96%D1%8B%D0%B3%D1%96%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%A1%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%8B_%28H._D%C3%BCrer%2C_1530%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1777" data-file-height="2423" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:%C5%BDygimont_Stary._%D0%96%D1%8B%D0%B3%D1%96%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%BD%D1%82_%D0%A1%D1%82%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%8B_(H._D%C3%BCrer,_1530).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Sigismund_I_the_Old" title="Sigismund I the Old">Sigismund Jagiellon</a>, one-time pretender to the Moldavian throne; 1530 portrait by <a href="/wiki/Hans_D%C3%BCrer" title="Hans Dürer">Hans Dürer</a></div></div></div>
<p>Researcher V. J. Parry argues that, because the Poles were continuously harassed by the Great Horde, they were in no position to help Stephen.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEParry197658_118-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEParry197658-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Eventually, in late 1486, Poland announced plans of actually starting a "crusade" against the Ottomans, to be led by <a href="/wiki/John_I_Albert" title="John I Albert">John Albert</a>; Stephen approached the <a href="/wiki/Sejm_of_the_Kingdom_of_Poland" title="Sejm of the Kingdom of Poland">Sejm</a> to negotiate Moldavia's role in the affair.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPilat2010129–130_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPilat2010129–130-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> He kept out, with the expedition being rerouted from Lviv, then attacking the Tatars.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPilat2010131–136_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPilat2010131–136-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> Poland concluded a peace treaty with the Ottoman Empire in 1489, acknowledging the loss of Chilia and Cetatea Albă, without Stephen's consent.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005270_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005270-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> Although the treaty confirmed Moldavia's frontiers, Stephen regarded it as a breach of his 1485 agreement with Casimir IV.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005270_147-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005270-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217_137-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> Instead of accepting the treaty, he acknowledged the suzerainty of Matthias Corvinus.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECernovodeanu1977118_123-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECernovodeanu1977118-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217_137-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049_148-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> However, Corvinus died unexpectedly on 6 April 1490.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049_148-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001317_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001317-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBain190830_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBain190830-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> Four candidates laid claim to Hungary, including <a href="/wiki/Maximilian_I,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Maximilian I, Holy Roman Emperor">Maximilian of Habsburg</a>, and Casimir IV's two sons, John Albert and <a href="/wiki/Vladislaus_II_of_Hungary" title="Vladislaus II of Hungary">Vladislaus</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049_148-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001345_151-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001345-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Stephen supported Maximilian of Habsburg, who urged the Three Nations of Transylvania to cooperate with Stephen against his opponents.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199657_104-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199657-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201461_152-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201461-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> Most Hungarian lords and prelates, however, supported Vladislaus who was crowned king on 21 September, forcing Maximilian to withdraw from Hungary in November.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001345–347_153-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001345–347-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> For John Albert (who was his father's heir in Poland) did not abandon his claim,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001347_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001347-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> Stephen decided to support Vladislaus in order to prevent a personal union between Hungary and Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217_137-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199663_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199663-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> He broke into Poland and captured Pocuția (now <a href="/wiki/Pokuttia" title="Pokuttia">Pokuttya</a> in Ukraine).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049_148-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201461_152-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201461-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199663_155-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199663-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> He believed that he was entitled to this former Moldavian fief, its revenue redirected toward paying the Ottoman tribute.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049_148-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> Stephen also supported Vladislaus against the Ottomans<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217_137-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECristea2016318–319_156-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECristea2016318–319-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> who broke into Hungary several times after Corvinus' death.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001359–360_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001359–360-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> In exchange, Vladislaus confirmed Stephen's claim to Ciceu and Cetatea de Baltă in Transylvania.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201462_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201462-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDiaconescu201397,_109_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDiaconescu201397,_109-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> John Albert, in turn, was forced to acknowledge his brother as the lawful king in late 1491.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217_137-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Casimir IV died on 7 June 1492.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost2015327_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost2015327-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> One of his younger sons, <a href="/wiki/Alexander_Jagiellon" title="Alexander Jagiellon">Alexander</a>, succeeded him in <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchy_of_Lithuania" title="Grand Duchy of Lithuania">Lithuania</a>, and John Albert was elected king of Poland in late August.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost2015327_160-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost2015327-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> Ivan III of Moscow broke into Lithuania to expand his authority over the <a href="/wiki/Upper_Oka_Principalities" title="Upper Oka Principalities">principalities along the borderlands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost2015283–284_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost2015283–284-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> During the following years, Ivan and Stephen coordinated their diplomacy, which enabled Ivan to persuade Alexander to acknowledge the loss of significant territories to Moscow in February 1494.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199664_162-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199664-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost2015285_163-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost2015285-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ottoman pressure also brought about a <a href="/wiki/Rapprochement" title="Rapprochement">rapprochement</a> between Hungary and Poland.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217_137-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001360_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001360-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup> Vladislaus met his four brothers, including John Albert and <a href="/wiki/Sigismund_I_the_Old" title="Sigismund I the Old">Sigismund</a>, in Lőcse (now <a href="/wiki/Levo%C4%8Da" title="Levoča">Levoča</a> in Slovakia) in April 1494.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201462_158-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201462-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENowakowska200746_165-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENowakowska200746-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> They planned a crusade against the Ottoman Empire.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENowakowska200746_165-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENowakowska200746-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> However, John Albert wanted to strengthen Polish suzerainty over Moldavia and to dethrone Stephen in favour of Sigismund, which gave rise to new tensions between Poland and Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost2015281_166-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost2015281-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217–218_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014217–218-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Shortly after the conference, John Albert decided to launch a campaign against the Ottomans to recapture Chilia and Cetatea Albă.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199664_162-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199664-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrost2015281_166-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrost2015281-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBain190843_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBain190843-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> Fearing that the subjugation of Moldavia was John Albert's actual purpose, Stephen made several attempts to prevent his campaign.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKohn2007_119-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKohn2007-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013137–138,_140_169-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013137–138,_140-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199665–66_170-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199665–66-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECristea2016315,_319_171-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECristea2016315,_319-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> With Ivan III's support, he persuaded Alexander of Lithuania not to associate himself with John Albert.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013137–138,_140_169-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013137–138,_140-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199666_172-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199666-172">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup> As reported by the <i><a href="/wiki/Bychowiec_Chronicle" title="Bychowiec Chronicle">Bychowiec Chronicle</a></i>, the Lithuanian magnates also condemned the war, and simply refused to cross the <a href="/wiki/Southern_Bug" title="Southern Bug">Southern Bug</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECristea2016319_173-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECristea2016319-173">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For its part, the Polish army <a href="/wiki/Moldavian_Campaign_(1497%E2%80%931499)" class="mw-redirect" title="Moldavian Campaign (1497–1499)">marched across the Dniester into Moldavia</a> in August 1497.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENowakowska2007132_174-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENowakowska2007132-174">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411_175-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> The Sultan sent 500 or 600 <a href="/wiki/Janissary" title="Janissary">Janissaries</a> to Moldavia at Stephen's request,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENowakowska2007132_174-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENowakowska2007132-174">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGemil201340_176-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGemil201340-176">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> joining the Moldavian forces gathered at <a href="/wiki/Roman,_Romania" title="Roman, Romania">Roman</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411_175-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> Stephen sent his chancellor, Isaac, to John Albert, requesting the withdrawal of Polish forces from Moldavia, but John Albert had Isaac imprisoned.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENowakowska2007132_174-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENowakowska2007132-174">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411_175-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> The Poles then laid siege to Suceava on 24 September.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBain190843_168-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBain190843-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411_175-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGrabarczyk2010_177-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrabarczyk2010-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> The campaign failed: Teutonic reinforcements never arrived, with <a href="/wiki/Johann_von_Tiefen" title="Johann von Tiefen">Johann von Tiefen</a> dying on the way.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMaasing2015362_129-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaasing2015362-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> Before long, a plague broke out in the Polish camp, while Vladislaus of Hungary sent an army of 12,000 strong to Moldavia, forcing John Albert to lift the siege on 19 October.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENowakowska2007132–133_178-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENowakowska2007132–133-178">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201463_179-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201463-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Poles started to march towards Poland, but Stephen <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Cosmin_Forest" title="Battle of the Cosmin Forest">ambushed and routed them at a ravine</a> in <a href="/wiki/Bukovina" title="Bukovina">Bukovina</a> on 25 and 26 October.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBain190843_168-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBain190843-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411_175-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGrabarczyk2010_177-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrabarczyk2010-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENowakowska2007133_180-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENowakowska2007133-180">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup> Several raids into Poland during the following months, including the sacking of Lviv, <a href="/wiki/Yavoriv" title="Yavoriv">Yavoriv</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Przemy%C5%9Bl" title="Przemyśl">Przemyśl</a>, cemented his victory. These were either ordered and directed by Stephen,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411_175-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200411-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014218_181-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014218-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199667_182-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199667-182">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiubotaru200569–71_183-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiubotaru200569–71-183">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> or carried out through a combined force of Ottoman–Tatar–Moldavian irregulars commanded by <a href="/wiki/Malko%C3%A7o%C4%9Flu_family" title="Malkoçoğlu family">Malkoçoğlu</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGorovei2014_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGorovei2014-184">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> Stephen made peace with John Albert only after Poland and Hungary concluded a new alliance against the Ottoman Empire,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014218_181-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014218-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> and Moldavia received direct access to Lviv's markets.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGorovei2014408–409_185-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGorovei2014408–409-185">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> Meanwhile, the Ottoman campaign ended in disaster, as a heavy winter induced famine; various Polish and Lithuanian reports also suggest that Stephen ordered <a href="/wiki/False_flag" title="False flag">false flag</a> attacks against his panicking former allies.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGorovei2014408_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGorovei2014408-186">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Last_years">Last years</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stephen_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Last years">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Stefanlespede.PNG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/Stefanlespede.PNG/220px-Stefanlespede.PNG" decoding="async" width="220" height="96" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/Stefanlespede.PNG/330px-Stefanlespede.PNG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/Stefanlespede.PNG/440px-Stefanlespede.PNG 2x" data-file-width="475" data-file-height="207" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Stefanlespede.PNG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The tomb of Stephen the Great and his wife, <a href="/wiki/Maria_Voichi%C8%9Ba" title="Maria Voichița">Maria Voichița</a> at <a href="/wiki/Putna_Monastery" title="Putna Monastery">Putna Monastery</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>From about 1498, power in Moldavia quietly shifted toward a group of boyars and administrators, comprising, among others, <a href="/wiki/Luca_Arbore" title="Luca Arbore">Luca Arbore</a> and Ioan Tăutu.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013136–137_187-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013136–137-187">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> Stephen's son and co-ruler, <a href="/wiki/Bogdan_III_the_One-Eyed" title="Bogdan III the One-Eyed">Bogdan</a>, was also taking on princely responsibilities from his father. He conducted the negotiations with Poland about a peace treaty.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201450_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201450-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> The treaty, which Stephen ratified at <a href="/wiki/H%C3%A2rl%C4%83u" title="Hârlău">Hârlău</a> in 1499, put an end to Polish suzerainty over Moldavia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997118_114-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBolovanConstantiniuMichelsonPop1997118-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201463_179-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201463-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> Stephen again stopped paying tribute to the Ottomans in 1500,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014218_181-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014218-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> although by then his health had declined. In February 1501, his delegation arrived in Venice, asking for a specialist doctor. As reported by <a href="/wiki/Marino_Sanuto_the_Younger" title="Marino Sanuto the Younger">Marin Sanudo</a>, his envoys also discussed the possibility of Moldavia and Hungary joining the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman%E2%80%93Venetian_War_(1499%E2%80%931503)" title="Ottoman–Venetian War (1499–1503)">Ottoman–Venetian War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMarin200985–87_189-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarin200985–87-189">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Doge_of_Venice" title="Doge of Venice">Doge of Venice</a>, <a href="/wiki/Agostino_Barbarigo" title="Agostino Barbarigo">Agostino Barbarigo</a>, sent a physician, Matteo Muriano, to Moldavia to treat his counterpart.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201450_188-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201450-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200412_190-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200412-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMarin200987–91_191-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarin200987–91-191">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Stephen's armies again broke into the Ottoman Empire, but they could not recapture Chilia or Cetatea Albǎ.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199659_127-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPapacostea199659-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014218_181-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014218-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> The Tatars of the Great Horde invaded southern Moldavia, but Stephen defeated them with the support of the Crimean Tatars in 1502.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219_192-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> He also sent reinforcements to Hungary to fight against the Ottomans.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219_192-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> By then, however, the treaty with Poland was no longer enforced, prompting Stephen to recapture Pocuția in 1502.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200412_190-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200412-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBain190852_193-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBain190852-193">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECiubotaru200571_194-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECiubotaru200571-194">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013139_195-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013139-195">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> Although Alexander of Lithuania was by then the new King of Poland, no understanding could be reached between him and Stephen, and the two became enemies.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013139_195-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013139-195">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> At around that time, Luca Arbore, acting either as Stephen's envoy or on his own, stated a Moldavian claim to <a href="/wiki/Halych" title="Halych">Halych</a> and other towns of the <a href="/wiki/Ruthenian_Voivodeship" title="Ruthenian Voivodeship">Ruthenian Voivodeship</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013138_196-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013138-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> Hungary and the Ottoman Empire concluded a new peace treaty on 22 February 1503, which also included Moldavia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEngel2001360_164-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEngel2001360-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219_192-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup> Thereafter Stephen again paid a yearly tribute to the Ottomans.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219_192-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Stephen survived his doctor, who died in Moldavia in late 1503.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200412_190-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200412-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMarin200987–91_191-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarin200987–91-191">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> Another Moldavian delegation was sent to Venice to ask for a replacement, but also to propose a new alliance against the Ottomans.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMarin200987–91_191-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarin200987–91-191">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> This was one of his last acts of international diplomacy. When Stephen was dying, various boyars, who opposed Bogdan, rebelled, but they were suppressed.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200412_190-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc200412-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201450,_219_197-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201450,_219-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008168_198-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008168-198">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> On his deathbed, he had urged Bogdan to continue to pay the tribute to the Sultan.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201463_179-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201463-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> He died on 2 July 1504 and was buried in the <a href="/wiki/Putna_Monastery" title="Putna Monastery">Monastery of Putna</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219_192-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044,_12_199-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044,_12-199">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939_200-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Family">Family</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stephen_the_Great&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Family">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>A woman named Mărușca (or Mărica) most probably gave birth to Stephen's first recognized son, <a href="/wiki/Alexandru_of_Moldavia" title="Alexandru of Moldavia">Alexandru</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201444_201-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201444-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008168–169_202-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008168–169-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938,_41,_45_203-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938,_41,_45-203">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup> Historian Ioan-Aurel Pop describes Mărușca as Stephen's first wife,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPop2005269_143-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPop2005269-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> but other researchers note that the legitimacy of the Stephen–Mărușca marriage is uncertain.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938_5-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201444_201-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201444-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008168–169_202-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008168–169-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> According to Jonathan Eagles, Alexandru either died in childhood or survived infancy and became his father's co-ruler.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201445_204-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201445-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> This older Alexandru died in July 1496, not before marrying a daughter of <a href="/wiki/Bartolomeu_Dragfi" title="Bartolomeu Dragfi">Bartolomeu Dragfi</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Voivode_of_Transylvania" title="Voivode of Transylvania">Transylvanian Voivode</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008168–169_202-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008168–169-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049,_10_205-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20049,_10-205">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196941,_45_206-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196941,_45-206">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> He is probably not the same Alexandru who, in 1486, was sent by Stephen as a voluntary hostage to Istanbul, where he married a Byzantine noblewoman.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008139–141,_159–160,_168–171_207-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008139–141,_159–160,_168–171-207">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup> This Alexandru was still alive by the end of his father's rule and beyond, when he became a pretender to the throne, and ultimately a contested prince.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008141,_168–170,_174–175_208-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008141,_168–170,_174–175-208">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> A 1538 letter by <a href="/wiki/Fabio_Mignanelli" title="Fabio Mignanelli">Fabio Mignanelli</a> describes the surviving Alexandru, or "Sandrin", as a posthumous son of Stephen, but this is likely an error.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008169_209-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008169-209">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>If Stephen fathered two or three sons named Alexandru, the one who was for a while his designated successor was born to <a href="/wiki/Evdochia_of_Kiev" title="Evdochia of Kiev">Evdochia of Kiev</a>, whom Stephen married in 1463.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044_3-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20044-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008168–169_202-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008168–169-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201445_204-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201445-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> An <a href="/wiki/Olelkovich" title="Olelkovich">Olelkovich</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938_5-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008102,_135_210-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008102,_135-210">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> she was closely related both to Ivan III of Moscow and to Casimir IV of Poland and Lithuania.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201445_204-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201445-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> Stephen's charter of grant to the <a href="/wiki/Hilandar" title="Hilandar">Hilandar Monastery</a> on <a href="/wiki/Mount_Athos" title="Mount Athos">Mount Athos</a> refers to two children of Stephen and Evdochia, Alexandru and <a href="/wiki/Elena_of_Moldavia" title="Elena of Moldavia">Olena</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPăun2016130–131_211-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPăun2016130–131-211">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> Olena was the wife of <a href="/wiki/Ivan_the_Young" title="Ivan the Young">Ivan Molodoy</a>, the eldest son of Ivan III, and mother of the usurped heir <a href="/wiki/Dmitry_Ivanovich_(grandson_of_Ivan_III)" title="Dmitry Ivanovich (grandson of Ivan III)">Dmitry</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048_113-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20048-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrezianuSpânu2007339_212-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrezianuSpânu2007339-212">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196945_213-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196945-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013138–139,_141_214-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEșanu2013138–139,_141-214">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Stephen's second (or third) wife, <a href="/wiki/Maria_of_Mangup" title="Maria of Mangup">Maria of Mangup</a>, was of the family of the <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Theodoro" title="Principality of Theodoro">Princes of Theodoro</a>. She was probably also cousins with the Muscovite Grand Princess <a href="/wiki/Sophia_Palaiologina" title="Sophia Palaiologina">Sophia Palaiologina</a> and was related to <a href="/wiki/Empire_of_Trebizond" title="Empire of Trebizond">Trebizond</a>'s royal couple, <a href="/wiki/David_of_Trebizond" title="David of Trebizond">Emperor David</a> and <a href="/wiki/Maria_of_Gothia" title="Maria of Gothia">Empress Maria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008136,_138_215-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008136,_138-215">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> The Stephen–Maria marriage took place in September 1472, but she died in December 1477.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008137–138_216-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008137–138-216">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045,_7_217-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20045,_7-217">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938–39_218-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938–39-218">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> During her brief stay in Moldavia, Maria supported the <a href="/wiki/Latin_Patriarchate_of_Constantinople" title="Latin Patriarchate of Constantinople">Latin Patriarchate of Constantinople</a>, contributing to the friendly contacts between Stephen and Catholic powers.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008137–139_219-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008137–139-219">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> Stephen's third (or fourth) wife, <a href="/wiki/Maria_Voichi%C8%9Ba" title="Maria Voichița">Maria Voichița</a>, was the daughter of Radu the Fair, Voivode of Wallachia. She was the mother of Stephen's immediate successor, Bogdan, and a daughter named <a href="/w/index.php?title=Maria_Cneajna&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Maria Cneajna (page does not exist)">Maria Cneajna</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://uk.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9C%D0%B0%D1%80%D1%96%D1%8F_%D0%A8%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%84%D0%B0%D0%BD%D1%96%D0%B2%D0%BD%D0%B0_%D0%92%D0%B8%D1%88%D0%BD%D0%B5%D0%B2%D0%B5%D1%86%D1%8C%D0%BA%D0%B0" class="extiw" title="uk:Марія Штефанівна Вишневецька">uk</a>&#93;</span>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201446,_50,_103_220-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201446,_50,_103-220">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939,_45,_47_221-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939,_45,_47-221">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> The latter married to Fedor Wiśniowiecki.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196947_222-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196947-222">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup> Stephen is known to have fathered two other sons who died in childhood, at a time when he was married to Maria Voichița: Bogdan died in 1479, and Peter (Petrașco) in 1480. Scholars are divided as to whether their mother was Evdochia,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles201445_204-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles201445-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> Maria of Mangup,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047_94-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDemciuc20047-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196945_213-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196945-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> or a very young Maria Voichița.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVoiculescu199185,_87_223-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVoiculescu199185,_87-223">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> Bogdan was also known as "Vlad"—a regal name rarely used in Moldavia, but common for Wallachian princes. The choice was either "an underlining of the Moldo–Wallachian unity which Prince Stephen had sought to achieve", or, more precisely but less certainly, a proof that Stephen wished to make Bogdan-Vlad his co-ruler over Wallachia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVoiculescu199187_224-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVoiculescu199187-224">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> Archivist Aurelian Sacerdoțeanu believes that Bogdan-Vlad also had a twin, Iliaș.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196945_213-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196945-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1480, Stephen finally recognized his first-born, Mircea, born from his 1450s affair with Călțuna of Brăila, and groomed him to take the throne in Wallachia. According to Sacerdoțeanu, recognition came only after the death of Mircea's legal father, who may have been one of the boyars spared at Soci.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938,_40–41_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196938,_40–41-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Stephen also fathered another illegitimate son, <a href="/wiki/Petru_Rare%C8%99" title="Petru Rareș">Petru Rareș</a>, who became prince of Moldavia in 1527.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219_192-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEagles2014219-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETreptowPopa1996160_225-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETreptowPopa1996160-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2017)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008172,_173,_175,_143_226-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMureșan2008172,_173,_175,_143-226">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939,_47_227-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939,_47-227">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> The church regards his mother, Maria Rareș, as Stephen's fourth wife,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEIliescu200679_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEIliescu200679-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> although she is known to have been married to a burgher.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939_200-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Stephen_V_of_Moldavia" title="Stephen V of Moldavia">Stephen V "Locust"</a>, who held the Moldavian throne in 1538–1540, also presented himself as Stephen's illegitimate son. According to Sacerdoțeanu, his claim is credible.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939,_47_227-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESacerdoțeanu196939,_47-227">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> A local tradition in <a href="/wiki/Putna_County" title="Putna County">Putna County</a> (today's <a href="/wiki/Vrancea_County" title="Vrancea County">Vrancea</a>) attributes to Stephen other extra-conjugal affairs, with many peasants reporting that they consider themselves "of his blood" or "of his marrow".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEIliescu200671_228-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEIliescu200671-228">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul class="gallery mw-gallery-traditional center">
